  # === TASK 12: Model Detail Page ===
  - id: "TASK-012"
    name: "Model Detail page with gallery and specs"
    description: |
      Build the Model Detail page — the most content-rich page on the site.
      
      Page: src/app/models/[slug]/page.tsx (dynamic route)
      
      Sections:
      
      1. **Hero**
         - Full-width hero image of the model
         - Model name, tagline, starting price
         - CTA: "Book a Test Drive" (scrolls to embedded form)
      
      2. **Image Gallery**
         - Tabbed gallery: Exterior, Interior, Detail, Lifestyle
         - Main large image + thumbnail strip below
         - Click thumbnail to change main image
         - Lightbox on click (full-screen view)
         - Lazy load images not in viewport
         - Component: src/components/models/ImageGallery.tsx
      
      3. **Specifications Table**
         - Grouped by category (Performance, Engine, Dimensions, etc.)
         - Accordion-style: click category to expand/collapse
         - Clean table layout within each category
         - Component: src/components/models/SpecsTable.tsx
      
      4. **Features Highlights**
         - Grid of feature cards grouped by category (Interior, Exterior, Technology, Safety)
         - Each card: icon (optional), title, description
         - Tab navigation between categories
         - Component: src/components/models/FeatureHighlights.tsx
      
      5. **Brochure Download**
         - If brochureUrl exists, show download CTA
         - Styled as a premium card with PDF icon
      
      6. **Embedded Test Drive Form**
         - The test drive form (from TASK-013) embedded at bottom
         - Model pre-selected based on current page
         - Anchor link from hero CTA scrolls here
      
      Implementation:
      - generateStaticParams for static generation of known model pages
      - generateMetadata for dynamic SEO (model name, description, image)
      - Server component with client islands for interactive parts (gallery, accordion, tabs)

  # === TASK 13: Test Drive Booking Form ===
  - id: "TASK-013"
    name: "Test Drive Booking form component"
    description: |
      Build the Test Drive Booking form — a key lead generation component.
      
      Component: src/components/forms/TestDriveForm.tsx
      
      Form fields:
      - First Name (text, required)
      - Last Name (text, required)
      - Email (email, required)
      - Phone (tel, required)
      - Preferred Model (select, required) — populated from /api/models
      - Dealership Location (select, required) — populated from /api/dealerships
      - Preferred Date (date picker, required, min = tomorrow)
      - GDPR Consent (checkbox, required) — "I agree to the processing of my personal data..."
      - Honeypot field (hidden 'website' input, visually hidden)
      
      Behavior:
      - Client-side validation using zod (share schemas from backend TASK-004)
      - Real-time field validation on blur
      - Form-level validation on submit
      - Submit to POST /api/bookings
      - Loading state during submission
      - Success state: "Thank you! We'll contact you within 24 hours." with checkmark animation
      - Error state: display server errors, allow retry
      - Reset form after successful submission (with delay for success message)
      
      Props:
      - preselectedModelId?: string — pre-select model (used on model detail page)
      - models: ModelSummary[] — available models for dropdown
      - dealerships: Dealership[] — available dealerships for dropdown
      
      Standalone page: src/app/book-test-drive/page.tsx
      - Full page version of the form
      - Hero section with "Book Your Test Drive" heading
      - Form centered on page
      - Also used embedded in model detail page (TASK-012)
      
      Implementation:
      - Use React Hook Form for form state management
      - Zod resolver for validation (share schemas with backend)
      - Fetch models and dealerships server-side, pass as props
      - Client component (needs interactivity)
      - Accessible: proper labels, error announcements, focus management
    agent: "frontend"
    status: "pending"
    priority: "high"
    inputs:
      requirements: "REQ-004"
      context:
        - "React Hook Form + Zod for validation"
        - "Share validation schemas with backend (import from src/lib/validations/forms.ts)"
        - "Honeypot field must be visually hidden but present in DOM"
        - "Pre-selected model when embedded on model detail page"
        - "Must work both standalone (/book-test-drive) and embedded"
      dependencies:
        - "TASK-007"
        - "TASK-004"
    outputs:
      artifacts:
        - "src/components/forms/TestDriveForm.tsx"
        - "src/app/book-test-drive/page.tsx"
    file_locks:
      - "src/components/forms/TestDriveForm.tsx"
      - "src/app/book-test-drive/"
    quality_gates:
      - "type_check"
      - "lint"
      - "build"
    human_checkpoint: false
    milestone: "Model Details & Forms"

  # === TASK 14: Contact Page ===
  - id: "TASK-014"
    name: "Contact page with form and map"
    description: |
      Build the Contact page with contact form, office info, and embedded map.
      
      Page: src/app/contact/page.tsx
      
      Sections:

         - Same validation pattern as test drive form (React Hook Form + Zod)
         - Submit to POST /api/contact
         - Success/error states
      
      4. **Contact Information**
         - Head office address (use Porsche AG, Porscheplatz 1, 70435 Stuttgart, Germany)
         - Phone number (placeholder)
         - Email (placeholder)
         - Business hours
      
      5. **Embedded Map**

         - Component: src/components/contact/MapEmbed.tsx

      6. **Social Media Links**
         - Icons for: Instagram, Facebook, Twitter/X, YouTube, LinkedIn
         - Placeholder URLs
         - Component: src/components/contact/SocialLinks.tsx
      
      Implementation:

      - Accessible form with proper labels and error handling

  # === TASK 15: Admin Dashboard UI ===
  - id: "TASK-015"
    name: "Admin dashboard UI"
    description: |
      Build the admin dashboard for reviewing leads and managing dealerships.
      
      Pages:
      
      1. **Login Page** (src/app/admin/login/page.tsx)
         - Simple email/password form
         - Uses NextAuth signIn()
         - Redirect to /admin on success
         - Error message on invalid credentials
      
      2. **Dashboard Home** (src/app/admin/page.tsx)
         - Stats cards: Total Bookings (new), Total Contacts (new), Active Models
         - Fetch from /api/admin/stats
         - Quick links to bookings and contacts lists
      
      3. **Bookings List** (src/app/admin/bookings/page.tsx)
         - Table: Date, Name, Email, Model, Dealership, Status, Actions
         - Status filter dropdown
         - Pagination controls
         - Click row to expand details
         - Status update dropdown (inline)
         - Notes field (expandable)
      
      4. **Contacts List** (src/app/admin/contacts/page.tsx)
         - Table: Date, Name, Email, Subject, Status, Actions
         - Status filter and pagination
         - Click to expand message
         - Status update
      
      5. **Dealerships Management** (src/app/admin/dealerships/page.tsx)
         - Table: Name, City, Country, Phone, Status, Actions
         - Add new dealership button → modal form
         - Edit dealership → modal form
         - Deactivate dealership (soft delete)
      
      Layout:
      - Admin layout (src/app/admin/layout.tsx) with sidebar navigation
      - Sidebar: Dashboard, Bookings, Contacts, Dealerships, Sign Out
      - Protected by NextAuth middleware — redirect to login if not authenticated
      - Light theme for admin (better for data-heavy UI) — white bg, dark text
      
      Implementation:
      - Client components for interactive tables
      - Fetch data from admin API routes
      - Use SWR or React Query for data fetching with revalidation
      - Modal component for dealership forms
      - Responsive: sidebar collapses to top nav on mobile

    status: "pending"
    priority: "high"

  # MILESTONE 4: Optimization & Polish (Week 4)

  # === TASK 17: SEO Implementation ===
  - id: "TASK-017"
    name: "SEO metadata, structured data, and sitemap"
    description: |
      Implement comprehensive SEO across all pages.
      
      Tasks:
      
      1. **Metadata per page** (using generateMetadata)
         - Homepage: brand name, description, OG image
         - Models list: "Explore Our Models | Porsche"
         - Model detail: dynamic — model name, description, hero image as OG
         - About: "Our Story | Porsche"
         - Contact: "Contact Us | Porsche"
         - Book Test Drive: "Book a Test Drive | Porsche"
         - All pages: canonical URL, OG tags, Twitter cards
      
      2. **Structured Data (JSON-LD)**
         - Homepage: Organization schema (name, logo, url, social links)
         - Model detail pages: Vehicle schema (Schema.org/Car)
           - name, description, image, brand, model, offers (price)
         - Contact page: LocalBusiness schema with address
         - Breadcrumb schema on all inner pages
         - Component: src/components/seo/JsonLd.tsx
      
      3. **Sitemap** (src/app/sitemap.ts)
         - Dynamic sitemap using Next.js sitemap generation
         - Include all static pages + all model detail pages
         - Set appropriate changeFrequency and priority
      
      4. **Robots.txt** (src/app/robots.ts)
         - Allow all public pages
         - Disallow /admin/*
         - Disallow /api/*
         - Reference sitemap URL
      
      5. **Meta component updates**
         - Ensure all pages have proper <title> and <meta description>
         - OG image fallback (create a default OG image or use brand image)
         - Viewport meta (should be in root layout already)
      
      Implementation:
      - Use Next.js App Router metadata API (generateMetadata, Metadata type)
      - JSON-LD as <script type="application/ld+json"> in page components
      - Validate structured data with Google's Rich Results Test format
    agent: "frontend"
    status: "pending"
    priority: "medium"
    inputs:
      requirements: "REQ-008"
      context:
        - "Use Next.js built-in metadata API"
        - "Dynamic metadata for model pages"
        - "Schema.org Vehicle markup for car pages"
        - "Sitemap must include dynamic model pages"
        - "Block admin and API routes from crawlers"
      dependencies:
        - "TASK-008"
        - "TASK-009"
        - "TASK-012"
        - "TASK-010"
        - "TASK-014"
    outputs:
      artifacts:
        - "src/components/seo/JsonLd.tsx"
        - "src/app/sitemap.ts"
        - "src/app/robots.ts"
    file_locks:

      - "lint"
      - "build"
    human_checkpoint: false
    milestone: "Optimization & Polish"

  # === TASK 18: Performance Optimization ===
  - id: "TASK-018"
    name: "Performance optimization"
    description: |
      Optimize the site for Lighthouse score above 85 across all categories.
      
      Optimizations:
      
      1. **Image Optimization**
         - Ensure all images use Next.js Image component with proper width/height/sizes
         - Add blur placeholder for above-fold images (blurDataURL)
         - Lazy load below-fold images (loading="lazy" — default in Next.js Image)
         - Use WebP format where possible (Next.js handles this automatically)
         - Proper srcSet/sizes for responsive images
         - Review all image components from previous tasks
      
      2. **Code Splitting**
         - Verify dynamic imports for heavy components (ImageGallery lightbox, admin components)
         - Use next/dynamic for components not needed on initial render
         - Lazy load ScrollReveal animations
         - Review bundle size with @next/bundle-analyzer
      
      3. **Font Optimization**
         - Use next/font for Outfit and Inter (automatic optimization)
         - Font display: swap
         - Preload critical fonts
         - Subset fonts if possible
      
      4. **CSS Optimization**
         - Tailwind purge is automatic — verify no unused styles
         - Critical CSS inlined by Next.js — verify
         - Remove any unused CSS imports
      
      5. **Core Web Vitals**
         - LCP: Ensure hero images are prioritized (priority prop on Next.js Image)
         - FID/INP: Minimize JavaScript on initial load
         - CLS: All images have explicit dimensions, no layout shifts
         - Add loading skeletons for dynamic content
      
      6. **Caching**
         - Verify Cache-Control headers on API routes
         - Static pages use ISR where appropriate
         - Assets cached by Vercel CDN automatically
      
      7. **Loading States**

project_id: "premium-car-brand-website"
project_name: "Premium Car Brand Marketing Website"
client: "Porsche"

global_context:
  tech_stack:
    framework: "Next.js 14 (App Router)"
    language: "TypeScript"
    styling: "Tailwind CSS"
    database: "PostgreSQL with Prisma ORM"
    auth: "NextAuth.js with Credentials provider"
    email: "Resend"
    analytics: "Google Analytics 4"
    deployment: "Vercel (human-managed)"
  design_direction: "Dark, premium aesthetic inspired by porsche.com/international/ — full-bleed imagery, clean sans-serif typography, generous whitespace, high contrast, subtle animations on scroll"
  vehicle_count: 3
  admin_auth: "Single admin user, credentials from ADMIN_EMAIL and ADMIN_PASSWORD env vars, no registration flow"
  dealership_locations: "Editable via admin UI"
  vehicle_data: "Research RapidAPI for car specs API. Design schema to support both API-sourced and manually-seeded data. Use seed data for development with 3 Porsche models."

# ─────────────────────────────────────────────
# MILESTONE 1: Foundation & Backend (Week 1)
# ─────────────────────────────────────────────
tasks:
  # --- M1: Foundation & Backend ---

  - id: "TASK-001"
    name: "Project scaffolding and configuration"
    description: |
      Initialize Next.js 14 App Router project with TypeScript, Tailwind CSS, Prisma ORM, and NextAuth.js.
      Set up project structure:
        - /src/app (App Router pages)
        - /src/components (shared UI components)
        - /src/lib (utilities, db client, auth config)
        - /src/types (shared TypeScript types)
        - /prisma (schema and migrations)
      Configure:
        - tailwind.config.ts with premium dark theme (colors: black, white, gold/amber accent, grays)
        - ESLint + Prettier
        - Environment variables template (.env.example) with all required vars
        - NextAuth.js configuration with Credentials provider (ADMIN_EMAIL, ADMIN_PASSWORD from env)
      Tech stack: Next.js 14, TypeScript, Tailwind CSS, Prisma, NextAuth.js, Resend
      Design inspiration: https://www.porsche.com/international/
    agent: "backend"
    status: "pending"
    priority: "critical"
    milestone: "Foundation & Backend"
    inputs:
      requirements: "REQ-006"
      context:
        - "This is the foundation task — all other tasks depend on it"
        - "Use App Router (not Pages Router)"
        - "Prisma with PostgreSQL"
        - "NextAuth Credentials provider with env-based admin credentials"
      dependencies: []
    outputs:
      artifacts:
        - "package.json"
        - "tsconfig.json"
        - "tailwind.config.ts"
        - "next.config.js"
        - "prisma/schema.prisma"
        - "src/lib/auth.ts"
        - "src/lib/db.ts"

        - "src/app/layout.tsx"

      - "src/lib/auth.ts"
      - "src/lib/db.ts"
      - ".env.example"
    quality_gates:
      - "type_check"
      - "lint"
      - "build"
    human_checkpoint: false

  - id: "TASK-002"
    name: "Database schema design and migration"
    description: |
      Design and implement the Prisma schema for the entire application:

      Tables:
      1. **VehicleModel** — id, slug, name, tagline, startingPrice, year, bodyType, heroImage, thumbnailImage, brochureUrl, order (for sorting), createdAt, updatedAt
      2. **VehicleSpecification** — id, modelId (FK), category (e.g. "Performance", "Dimensions"), label, value, unit, order
      3. **VehicleImage** — id, modelId (FK), url, alt, category (exterior/interior/detail), order
      4. **VehicleFeature** — id, modelId (FK), category (technology/safety/comfort), title, description, icon
      5. **TestDriveBooking** — id, firstName, lastName, email, phone, preferredModelId (FK nullable), dealershipId (FK), preferredDate, gdprConsent, status (new/contacted/completed/cancelled), notes, createdAt
      6. **ContactSubmission** — id, firstName, lastName, email, subject, message, gdprConsent, status (new/read/replied), createdAt
      7. **Dealership** — id, name, address, city, state, postalCode, country, phone, email, latitude, longitude, isActive, createdAt, updatedAt

      Create seed script with 3 Porsche models (911, Taycan, Cayenne) with realistic specs, features, and placeholder image URLs.
      Create seed data for 3-5 dealership locations.

      Run migration and verify schema.
    agent: "backend"
    status: "pending"
    priority: "critical"
    milestone: "Foundation & Backend"
    inputs:
      requirements: "REQ-006"
      context:

        - "3 Porsche models at launch: 911, Taycan, Cayenne"
        - "Dealerships are editable via admin, so they need a database table"

    human_checkpoint: false

  - id: "TASK-003"
    name: "Vehicle Models API endpoints"
    description: |
      Implement REST API endpoints for vehicle models:

      1. **GET /api/models** — List all models with thumbnail, name, tagline, startingPrice, bodyType, slug. Support optional query params: ?bodyType=suv. Ordered by `order` field.
      2. **GET /api/models/[slug]** — Full model detail including all specifications, images, and features. Return 404 if not found.

      Both endpoints should:
      - Use Prisma client from src/lib/db.ts
      - Return proper HTTP status codes
      - Include appropriate cache headers (revalidate every 60s for list, 300s for detail)
      - Handle errors gracefully with consistent error response format: { error: string, status: number }
      - Use TypeScript types for all request/response shapes

      Create shared types in src/types/api.ts for API response shapes.
    agent: "backend"

    priority: "high"

  - id: "TASK-004"
    name: "Form submission API endpoints"
    description: |
      Implement API endpoints for form submissions:

      1. **POST /api/bookings** — Test drive booking submission
         - Validate all required fields (firstName, lastName, email, phone, preferredModelId, dealershipId, preferredDate, gdprConsent)
         - gdprConsent must be true or reject with 400
         - Validate email format
         - Rate limit: max 5 submissions per IP per hour (use simple in-memory store, not Redis)
         - Honeypot field: if `website` field is filled, silently return 200 (spam trap)
         - Store in database with status "new"
         - Send notification email via Resend to admin email (from env var ADMIN_NOTIFICATION_EMAIL)
         - Return 201 on success

      2. **POST /api/contact** — Contact form submission
         - Validate: firstName, lastName, email, subject, message, gdprConsent
         - Same spam protection (honeypot + rate limit)
         - Store in database with status "new"
         - Send notification email via Resend
         - Return 201 on success

      3. **GET /api/dealerships** — List active dealerships
         - Return all dealerships where isActive=true
         - Ordered by name
         - Used by test drive form for location dropdown

      Create Resend email utility in src/lib/email.ts with graceful fallback if RESEND_API_KEY not set (log to console instead).
    agent: "backend"
    status: "pending"
    priority: "high"
    milestone: "Foundation & Backend"

      - "lint"
      - "unit_tests"

  - id: "TASK-005"
    name: "Admin API endpoints and authentication"
    description: |
      Implement admin-only API endpoints protected by NextAuth session:

      1. **GET /api/admin/bookings** — List all test drive bookings
         - Paginated: ?page=1&limit=20
         - Filterable: ?status=new
         - Sorted by createdAt desc
         - Include related model name and dealership name

      2. **PATCH /api/admin/bookings/[id]** — Update booking status and notes
         - Validate status is one of: new, contacted, completed, cancelled
         - Optional notes field

      3. **GET /api/admin/contacts** — List all contact submissions
         - Paginated: ?page=1&limit=20
         - Filterable: ?status=new
         - Sorted by createdAt desc

      4. **PATCH /api/admin/contacts/[id]** — Update contact status
         - Validate status is one of: new, read, replied

      5. **GET /api/admin/dealerships** — List all dealerships (including inactive)
      6. **POST /api/admin/dealerships** — Create dealership
      7. **PATCH /api/admin/dealerships/[id]** — Update dealership
      8. **DELETE /api/admin/dealerships/[id]** — Soft delete (set isActive=false)

      All /api/admin/* routes must check for valid NextAuth session. Return 401 if not authenticated.
      Create middleware helper: src/lib/admin-auth.ts with getAdminSession() that throws if not authenticated.
    agent: "backend"
    status: "pending"
    priority: "high"
    milestone: "Foundation & Backend"
    inputs:
      requirements: "REQ-006"
      context:
        - "Admin auth: NextAuth Credentials provider, single admin user from env vars"
        - "Dealerships are CRUD-able by admin (human requested editable by team)"
        - "Pagination with page/limit pattern"
        - "All admin routes require authentication"
      dependencies:
        - "TASK-002"
        - "TASK-001"
    outputs:
      artifacts:
        - "src/app/api/admin/bookings/route.ts"
        - "src/app/api/admin/bookings/[id]/route.ts"
        - "src/app/api/admin/contacts/route.ts"
        - "src/app/api/admin/contacts/[id]/route.ts"
        - "src/app/api/admin/dealerships/route.ts"
        - "src/app/api/admin/dealerships/[id]/route.ts"
        - "src/lib/admin-auth.ts"
    file_locks:
      - "src/app/api/admin/bookings/route.ts"
      - "src/app/api/admin/bookings/[id]/route.ts"
      - "src/app/api/admin/contacts/route.ts"
      - "src/app/api/admin/contacts/[id]/route.ts"
      - "src/app/api/admin/dealerships/route.ts"
      - "src/app/api/admin/dealerships/[id]/route.ts"
      - "src/lib/admin-auth.ts"
    quality_gates:
      - "type_check"
      - "lint"
      - "unit_tests"
    human_checkpoint: false

  - id: "TASK-006"
    name: "Backend unit tests for Milestone 1"
    description: |
      Write comprehensive unit tests for all backend API endpoints:

      Test files:
      1. **tests/api/models.test.ts** — Test GET /api/models (list, filtering), GET /api/models/[slug] (found, not found)
      2. **tests/api/bookings.test.ts** — Test POST /api/bookings (valid, missing fields, invalid email, gdpr false, honeypot, rate limit)
      3. **tests/api/contact.test.ts** — Test POST /api/contact (valid, missing fields, honeypot)
      4. **tests/api/dealerships.test.ts** — Test GET /api/dealerships (returns active only)
      5. **tests/api/admin.test.ts** — Test all admin endpoints (authenticated, unauthenticated, pagination, status updates, dealership CRUD)

      Use:
      - Jest or Vitest (whichever is configured in TASK-001)
      - Mock Prisma client
      - Mock NextAuth session for admin tests
      - Mock Resend for email tests

      Target: 90%+ coverage on API route handlers.
    agent: "qa"
    status: "pending"
    priority: "high"
    milestone: "Foundation & Backend"
    inputs:
      requirements: "REQ-006"
      context:
        - "Test all API endpoints from TASK-003, TASK-004, TASK-005"
        - "Mock database and external services"
        - "Test both happy path and error cases"
        - "Test spam protection (honeypot, rate limiting)"
        - "Test auth protection on admin routes"
      dependencies:
        - "TASK-003"
        - "TASK-004"
        - "TASK-005"
    outputs:
      artifacts:
        - "tests/api/models.test.ts"
        - "tests/api/bookings.test.ts"
        - "tests/api/contact.test.ts"
        - "tests/api/dealerships.test.ts"
        - "tests/api/admin.test.ts"
        - "tests/setup.ts"
    file_locks:
      - "tests/api/models.test.ts"
      - "tests/api/bookings.test.ts"
      - "tests/api/contact.test.ts"
      - "tests/api/dealerships.test.ts"
      - "tests/api/admin.test.ts"
      - "tests/setup.ts"

      - "unit_tests"
    human_checkpoint: false

  # --- M2: Core Pages (Week 2) ---

  - id: "TASK-007"
    name: "Design system and shared UI components"
    description: |
      Create the foundational design system and shared components inspired by porsche.com:

      Design tokens in tailwind.config.ts (extend existing):
      - Colors: primary black (#0A0A0A), white (#FFFFFF), accent gold (#C4A265), grays (50-900), error red, success green
      - Typography: Font family (use Inter for body, Outfit or similar for headings), sizes, weights
      - Spacing: Consistent scale
      - Breakpoints: sm(640), md(768), lg(1024), xl(1280), 2xl(1536)

      Shared components (src/components/ui/):
      1. **Button** — Primary (gold bg), Secondary (outlined), Ghost. Sizes: sm, md, lg. Loading state.
      2. **Container** — Max-width wrapper with responsive padding
      3. **SectionHeading** — Overline text + heading + optional subheading
      4. **Card** — Image + content card with hover effect (scale + shadow)
      5. **Input** — Styled form input with label, error state, helper text
      6. **Textarea** — Styled textarea matching Input
      7. **Select** — Styled select dropdown matching Input
      8. **Checkbox** — Styled checkbox with label (for GDPR consent)
      9. **Badge** — Status badges (for admin: new, contacted, completed, etc.)
      10. **LoadingSpinner** — Animated spinner
      11. **Navbar** — Fixed top navigation with logo, links (Models, About, Contact), CTA button (Book Test Drive). Mobile hamburger menu.
      12. **Footer** — Brand info, navigation links, social links, copyright. Dark background.

      All components must:
      - Be fully typed with TypeScript props interfaces
      - Support className override via cn() utility (clsx + tailwind-merge)
      - Be accessible (proper ARIA attributes, keyboard navigation)
      - Include hover/focus/active states

      Create cn() utility in src/lib/utils.ts
    agent: "frontend"
    status: "pending"
    priority: "critical"
    milestone: "Core Pages"
    inputs:
      requirements: "REQ-001, REQ-007"
      context:
        - "Design inspired by porsche.com/international/ — dark, premium, minimal"
        - "These components are used across ALL pages"
        - "Must be accessible and responsive"
        - "Use Tailwind CSS only — no component library"
      dependencies:
        - "TASK-001"
    outputs:
      artifacts:
        - "src/components/ui/button.tsx"
        - "src/components/ui/container.tsx"
        - "src/components/ui/section-heading.tsx"
        - "src/components/ui/card.tsx"
        - "src/components/ui/input.tsx"
        - "src/components/ui/textarea.tsx"
        - "src/components/ui/select.tsx"
        - "src/components/ui/checkbox.tsx"
        - "src/components/ui/badge.tsx"
        - "src/components/ui/loading-spinner.tsx"
        - "src/components/layout/navbar.tsx"
        - "src/components/layout/footer.tsx"
        - "src/lib/utils.ts"
    file_locks:
      - "src/components/ui/*"
      - "src/components/layout/navbar.tsx"
      - "src/components/layout/footer.tsx"
      - "src/lib/utils.ts"
    quality_gates:
      - "type_check"
      - "lint"
      - "build"
    human_checkpoint: false

  - id: "TASK-008"
    name: "Homepage implementation"
    description: |
      Build the homepage (src/app/page.tsx) with the following sections:

      1. **Hero Section** — Full-viewport height, dark background with large hero image (use placeholder Porsche image URL). Overlay with brand tagline ("Engineered for the Extraordinary" or similar), sub-text, and two CTA buttons: "Explore Models" (link to /models) and "Book a Test Drive" (link to /book-test-drive). Subtle scroll-down indicator animation.

      2. **Featured Models Section** — Heading "Our Models", 3 model cards in responsive grid fetched from GET /api/models. Each card shows image, model name, tagline, starting price, and "Discover" link to /models/[slug]. Cards have hover zoom effect on image.

      3. **Brand Story Teaser** — Split layout: image on one side, text on other. Brief brand story text with "Learn More" link to /about. Dark background section.

      4. **CTA Banner** — Full-width section with background image, heading "Experience the Drive", subtext, and "Book a Test Drive" button.

      Page must:
      - Use server components for data fetching (fetch models on server)
      - Include proper metadata (title, description, OG tags)
      - Be fully responsive
      - Use shared components from TASK-007
      - Have smooth scroll animations (use CSS or lightweight intersection observer)
    agent: "frontend"
    status: "pending"
    priority: "high"
    milestone: "Core Pages"
    inputs:
      requirements: "REQ-001"
      context:
        - "Server component — fetch models from API on server side"
        - "Dark, premium aesthetic — porsche.com inspiration"
        - "3 featured models from database"
        - "Full-viewport hero with CTA buttons"
      dependencies:
        - "TASK-007"
        - "TASK-003"
    outputs:
      artifacts:
        - "src/app/page.tsx"
        - "src/components/home/hero-section.tsx"
        - "src/components/home/featured-models.tsx"
        - "src/components/home/brand-teaser.tsx"
        - "src/components/home/cta-banner.tsx"
    file_locks:

      - "src/components/home/*"
    quality_gates:
      - "type_check"
      - "lint"
      - "build"
    human_checkpoint: false

    name: "Model Overview page"
    description: |
      Build the Model Overview page (src/app/models/page.tsx):

      - Fetch all models from GET /api/models (server component)
      - Page heading with "Our Models" title and subtitle
      - Responsive grid layout: 1 column mobile, 2 tablet, 3 desktop
      - Each model card shows: hero image, name, tagline, starting price, body type badge, "Explore" link

    file_locks:

    human_checkpoint: false

    description: |
      Build the About page (src/app/about/page.tsx):

      Sections:
      1. **Hero** — Full-width image with "Our Story" heading overlay
      2. **Brand Story** — Rich text section with Porsche brand narrative (use realistic placeholder copy about engineering excellence, racing heritage, innovation)
      3. **Mission & Vision** — Two-column layout with mission and vision statements
      4. **Innovation Commitment** — Section highlighting technology leadership with icon cards
      5. **Timeline** — Visual vertical timeline of key Porsche milestones:

         - 1996: Boxster launch
         - 2019: Taycan — first all-electric Porsche

      Page must:

      - Include proper metadata
      - Use premium typography and spacing

    agent: "frontend"

        - "src/components/about/timeline.tsx"
        - "src/components/about/mission-vision.tsx"
    file_locks:

      - "src/components/about/*"
    quality_gates:

  - id: "TASK-011"

    description: |
      Write component tests for all Milestone 2 pages and components:

      1. **tests/components/ui/** — Test shared UI components (Button renders variants, Input shows error state, Navbar mobile toggle, etc.)
      2. **tests/pages/home.test.tsx** — Homepage renders hero, featured models, CTAs
      3. **tests/pages/models.test.tsx** — Model overview renders grid, handles empty state

  # --- M3: Model Details & Forms (Week 3) ---

  - id: "TASK-012"
    name: "Model Detail page with gallery and specs"
    description: |
      Build the Model Detail page (src/app/models/[slug]/page.tsx):

      Sections:
      1. **Hero** — Full-width hero image of the model with model name and tagline overlay
      2. **Key Stats Bar** — Horizontal bar showing 3-4 key specs (e.g., 0-60 time, top speed, horsepower, range for EV)
      3. **Image Gallery** — Grid/slider of model images with lightbox on click. Categories: exterior, interior, detail. Lazy loaded.
      4. **Specifications Table** — Grouped by category (Performance, Dimensions, Engine/Motor, etc.). Clean table layout.
      5. **Features** — Card grid of technology, safety, and comfort features with icons
      6. **Brochure Download** — CTA to download PDF brochure (if brochureUrl exists)
      7. **Test Drive CTA** — Embedded test drive booking form OR prominent CTA linking to /book-test-drive?model=[slug]

      Page must:
      - Use generateStaticParams for static generation of known model slugs
      - Fetch from GET /api/models/[slug] (server component)
      - Handle 404 (notFound()) if model doesn't exist
      - Include dynamic metadata (model name in title, description)
      - Images lazy loaded with blur placeholder

      Create reusable components:
      - src/components/models/image-gallery.tsx (with lightbox)
      - src/components/models/specs-table.tsx
      - src/components/models/features-grid.tsx
      - src/components/models/key-stats.tsx
    agent: "frontend"
    status: "pending"
    priority: "high"
    milestone: "Model Details & Forms"
    inputs:
      requirements: "REQ-003"
      context:
        - "Dynamic route with generateStaticParams"
        - "Server component fetching full model data"
        - "Image gallery with lightbox — keep it lightweight"
        - "Specs grouped by category"
        - "Link to test drive form with model pre-selected"
      dependencies:
        - "TASK-007"
        - "TASK-003"
    outputs:

        - "src/components/models/specs-table.tsx"
        - "src/components/models/features-grid.tsx"
        - "src/components/models/key-stats.tsx"

  - id: "TASK-013"
    name: "Test Drive Booking form page"
    description: |
      Build the Test Drive Booking page (src/app/book-test-drive/page.tsx):

      Form fields:
      - First Name (required)
      - Last Name (required)
      - Email (required, validated)
      - Phone (required)
      - Preferred Model (select dropdown, populated from GET /api/models, pre-selected if ?model= query param)
      - Dealership Location (select dropdown, populated from GET /api/dealerships)
      - Preferred Date (date picker, min: tomorrow, max: 90 days out)
      - GDPR Consent checkbox (required, with link to privacy policy)
      - Hidden honeypot field (name="website", hidden via CSS not display:none)

      Behavior:
      - Client component with form state management (React Hook Form or native)
      - Client-side validation with clear error messages
      - Submit to POST /api/bookings
      - Loading state on submit button
      - Success state: show confirmation message with booking details
      - Error state: show error message, allow retry
      - Pre-populate model from URL query param

      Design:
      - Split layout: form on left, aspirational image on right (desktop)
      - Full-width form on mobile
      - Premium styling matching site aesthetic
    agent: "frontend"
    status: "pending"
    priority: "high"
    milestone: "Model Details & Forms"
    inputs:
      requirements: "REQ-004"
      context:
        - "Client component for form interactivity"
        - "Fetch models and dealerships for dropdowns"
        - "Honeypot field hidden via CSS (not display:none — bots detect that)"
        - "GDPR consent mandatory"
        - "Pre-select model from URL query param"
      dependencies:
        - "TASK-007"
        - "TASK-003"
        - "TASK-004"
    outputs:

      - "lint"
      - "build"
    human_checkpoint: false

    name: "Contact page with form and map"

      Contact Form:

    file_locks:

  # --- M3.5: Admin UI (part of Foundation but depends on backend) ---

  - id: "TASK-016"
    name: "Admin dashboard UI"
    description: |
      Build the admin dashboard (src/app/admin/):

      Pages:
      1. **Login page** (src/app/admin/login/page.tsx)
         - Email and password fields
         - Submit via NextAuth signIn("credentials")
         - Redirect to /admin on success
         - Show error on invalid credentials

      2. **Dashboard** (src/app/admin/page.tsx)
         - Summary cards: total bookings, new bookings, total contacts, new contacts
         - Recent submissions list (last 5 of each)
         - Quick links to full lists

      3. **Bookings list** (src/app/admin/bookings/page.tsx)
         - Table with: date, name, email, model, dealership, status, actions
         - Filter by status dropdown
         - Pagination
         - Click row to expand details
         - Status update dropdown (new → contacted → completed/cancelled)
         - Notes field (editable inline)

      4. **Contacts list** (src/app/admin/contacts/page.tsx)
         - Table with: date, name, email, subject, status, actions
         - Filter by status
         - Pagination
         - Click to expand full message
         - Status update (new → read → replied)

      5. **Dealerships management** (src/app/admin/dealerships/page.tsx)
         - Table of all dealerships
         - Add new dealership button → modal/form
         - Edit dealership → modal/form
         - Toggle active/inactive
         - Delete (with confirmation)

      Layout:
      - Admin layout (src/app/admin/layout.tsx) with sidebar navigation
      - Sidebar: Dashboard, Bookings, Contacts, Dealerships, Sign Out
      - Light theme for admin (contrast with dark public site)
      - Protected by NextAuth middleware — redirect to /admin/login if not authenticated

      Create middleware.ts at project root to protect /admin/* routes.
    agent: "frontend"
    status: "pending"
    priority: "high"
    milestone: "Foundation & Backend"
    inputs:

  # --- M4: Optimization & Polish (Week 4) ---

  - id: "TASK-017"
    name: "SEO metadata and structured data"
    description: |
      Implement comprehensive SEO across all pages:

      1. **Root layout metadata** (update src/app/layout.tsx)
         - Default title template: "%s | Porsche"
         - Default description, OG image, Twitter card
         - Viewport, theme-color, favicon

      2. **Per-page metadata** using generateMetadata:
         - Homepage: "Porsche | Engineered for the Extraordinary"
         - Models: "Our Models | Porsche"
         - Model Detail: "[Model Name] | Porsche" with dynamic description and OG image
         - About: "Our Story | Porsche"
         - Contact: "Contact Us | Porsche"
         - Book Test Drive: "Book a Test Drive | Porsche"

      3. **Structured data** (JSON-LD):
         - Organization schema on homepage
         - Vehicle schema on model detail pages (Schema.org/Vehicle)
         - BreadcrumbList on all pages
         - LocalBusiness on contact page

      4. **Technical SEO**:
         - Generate sitemap.xml (src/app/sitemap.ts)
         - Generate robots.txt (src/app/robots.ts)
         - Canonical URLs on all pages
         - Proper heading hierarchy (h1 → h2 → h3)

      Create utility: src/lib/structured-data.ts for generating JSON-LD objects.

    status: "pending"
    priority: "medium"

  - id: "TASK-018"
    name: "Performance optimization"
    description: |
      Optimize site performance targeting Lighthouse score above 85:

      1. **Image optimization**:
         - Ensure all images use Next.js Image component with proper width/height/sizes
         - Add blur placeholders for hero images (use plaiceholder or static blurDataURL)
         - Convert to WebP format where possible
         - Implement responsive srcSet for different viewport sizes

      2. **Lazy loading**:
         - Below-fold sections loaded with Intersection Observer
         - Image gallery images lazy loaded
         - Non-critical components dynamically imported with next/dynamic

      3. **Code splitting**:
         - Dynamic imports for heavy components (image gallery lightbox, date picker, map embed)
         - Analyze bundle with @next/bundle-analyzer
         - Remove unused dependencies

      4. **CSS optimization**:
         - Ensure Tailwind purges unused styles (should be default)
         - Critical CSS inlined by Next.js (verify)

      5. **Caching**:
         - Verify API route cache headers are set correctly
         - Static pages use ISR where appropriate
         - Add Cache-Control headers for static assets

      6. **Loading states**:
         - Add loading.tsx files for route segments that fetch data
         - Skeleton screens for model cards and detail page

      Create loading.tsx files:
      - src/app/models/loading.tsx
      - src/app/models/[slug]/loading.tsx
    agent: "frontend"
    status: "pending"
    priority: "medium"
    milestone: "Optimization & Polish"
    inputs:
      requirements: "REQ-009"
      context:
        - "Target Lighthouse 85+"
        - "Next.js Image component for all images"
        - "Dynamic imports for heavy components"
        - "Loading skeletons for data-fetching routes"
      dependencies:
        - "TASK-008"
        - "TASK-009"
        - "TASK-012"
    outputs:

        - "src/components/ui/skeleton.tsx"
    file_locks:

      - "src/components/ui/skeleton.tsx"
    quality_gates:

      - "lint"
      - "build"
    human_checkpoint: false

  - id: "TASK-019"
    name: "SEO and performance tests"
    description: |
      Write tests to validate SEO and performance optimizations:

      1. **tests/seo/metadata.test.ts**
         - Verify each page has correct title, description, OG tags
         - Verify model detail pages have dynamic metadata

      4. **tests/accessibility/a11y.test.ts**

         - Verify form labels and ARIA attributes
         - Verify keyboard navigation on interactive elements

  # --- M5: QA & Deployment Prep (Week 5) ---

  - id: "TASK-020"
    name: "End-to-end test suite"
    description: |
      Write end-to-end tests using Playwright:

      Test scenarios:
      1. **Navigation flow** — Visit homepage → click "Explore Models" → see models grid → click a model → see detail page → click "Book Test Drive" → see form
      2. **Test drive booking flow** — Fill form → submit → see confirmation
      3. **Contact form flow** — Fill form → submit → see confirmation
      4. **Model browsing** — View all models → click each → verify content loads
      5. **Admin login flow** — Visit /admin → redirected to login → login → see dashboard
      6. **Admin booking management** — Login → view bookings → change status → verify update
      7. **Admin dealership management** — Login → add dealership → edit → deactivate
      8. **Responsive checks** — Run key flows at mobile (375px), tablet (768px), desktop (1280px) viewports
      9. **404 handling** — Visit /models/nonexistent → see 404 page

      Configure Playwright:
      - playwright.config.ts at project root
      - Test against local dev server
      - Screenshots on failure
      - HTML report generation

         - trackCTAClick(ctaName, location) — CTA button clicks

    question: |
      MANUAL WORK REQUIRED: Deployment Configuration

      The devops agent is not yet available. The following deployment tasks need to be handled manually or deferred:

      1. Vercel project setup and configuration
      2. PostgreSQL database provisioning (e.g., Vercel Postgres, Supabase, or Neon)
      3. Environment variable configuration in Vercel dashboard
      4. Domain configuration
      5. CI/CD pipeline (if desired beyond Vercel's built-in)

      The project will include a comprehensive .env.example and README with all required configuration.

      When would you like to handle deployment setup?
      1. After Milestone 1 (so we can test with real database early)
      2. After Milestone 3 (when core features are complete)
      3. After Milestone 5 (at the very end)
      4. I'll handle it myself — just give me the docs
    priority: "high"
    category: "manual_work_required"
    blocking_tasks: []

      requirements: |
        Design and implement the PostgreSQL database schema using Prisma ORM.
        
        TABLES REQUIRED:
        
        1. **VehicleModel**
           - id (UUID, primary key)
           - name (string, e.g., "911 Carrera")
           - slug (string, unique, URL-friendly)
           - tagline (string, short marketing line)
           - description (text, full marketing description)
           - startingPrice (decimal)
           - year (int)
           - category (enum: SPORTS, SUV, SEDAN, ELECTRIC)
           - heroImage (string, URL)
           - isActive (boolean, default true)
           - sortOrder (int, for display ordering)
           - createdAt, updatedAt timestamps
        
        2. **VehicleSpecification**
           - id (UUID)
           - modelId (FK to VehicleModel)
           - category (string, e.g., "Performance", "Dimensions", "Engine")
           - label (string, e.g., "Top Speed")
           - value (string, e.g., "182 mph")
           - sortOrder (int)
        
        3. **VehicleImage**
           - id (UUID)
           - modelId (FK to VehicleModel)
           - url (string)
           - alt (string)
           - category (enum: EXTERIOR, INTERIOR, DETAIL, GALLERY)
           - isPrimary (boolean)
           - sortOrder (int)
        
        4. **VehicleHighlight**
           - id (UUID)
           - modelId (FK to VehicleModel)
           - title (string)
           - description (text)
           - icon (string, optional)
           - category (enum: EXTERIOR, INTERIOR, TECHNOLOGY, SAFETY)
           - sortOrder (int)
        
        5. **TestDriveBooking**
           - id (UUID)
           - firstName, lastName (strings)
           - email (string)
           - phone (string)
           - modelId (FK to VehicleModel)
           - dealershipId (FK to Dealership)
           - preferredDate (date)
           - message (text, optional)
           - gdprConsent (boolean)
           - status (enum: NEW, CONTACTED, SCHEDULED, COMPLETED, CANCELLED)
           - createdAt, updatedAt
        
        6. **ContactSubmission**
           - id (UUID)
           - firstName, lastName (strings)
           - email (string)
           - subject (string)
           - message (text)
           - gdprConsent (boolean)
           - status (enum: NEW, READ, REPLIED, ARCHIVED)
           - createdAt, updatedAt
        
        7. **Dealership**
           - id (UUID)
           - name (string)
           - address (string)
           - city (string)
           - state (string, optional)
           - country (string)
           - postalCode (string)
           - phone (string)
           - email (string)
           - latitude (float)
           - longitude (float)
           - isActive (boolean)
           - sortOrder (int)
        
        ALSO REQUIRED:
        - Prisma client singleton (src/lib/db.ts)
        - Seed script with 3 Porsche models (911, Taycan, Cayenne) with realistic specs,

        - Seed 3 dealership locations
        - TypeScript types exported from src/types/models.ts (derived from Prisma but

        - Check RapidAPI for a car specifications API that could provide Porsche model data

           - Icon support (left/right)

           - Variants: default (max-w-7xl), narrow (max-w-4xl), wide (max-w-screen-2xl), full
        
        3. **SectionHeading** — Consistent section title treatment
           - Eyebrow text (small, uppercase, accent color)
           - Main heading (large, Outfit font)
           - Subtitle (muted text)
           - Alignment: left, center
        
        4. **Card** — Dark card with hover effect

           - Legal links (Privacy, Terms)

            - Direction variants: up, down, left, right, fade

        - Every component must use forwardRef where appropriate

      { status: 500 }
    );
  }
}

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   The user asked me to investigate what's wrong with their SoloEnterprise project based on extensive orchestrator logs from a "Premium Car Brand Marketing Website" build that was cancelled. The logs showed the orchestrator agent repeatedly failing to produce actionable commands across 4 attempts, and then crashing with a PostgreSQL error. I am in **plan mode** (read-only except for plan file at `/Users/matteobaratella/.claude/plans/quirky-nibbling-swan.md`).

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - SoloEnterprise: AI agent orchestration system with human-in-the-loop feedback
   - Orchestrator agent produces YAML output that gets parsed into tasks, questions, status updates, and file locks
   - Claude API streaming with max_tokens limit (16384)
   - Drizzle ORM with Neon PostgreSQL (UUID primary keys)
   - YAML extraction and parsing pipeline for orchestrator output
   - UUID prefix resolution for task ID references
   - PostgreSQL type system (UUID type doesn't support LIKE/`~~` operator)

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - **`packages/core/src/agents/utils/orchestrator-command-executor.ts`** (lines 40-80, 295-320)
     - Contains `resolveTaskIdWithLookup()` function that uses `like()` on UUID columns
     - **Bug 2 root cause**: Lines 59 and 310 use `like(tasks.id, \`${taskId}%\`)` which fails because PostgreSQL UUID type doesn't support the `~~` (LIKE) operator
     ```typescript
     // Line 59 - BUG: like() on UUID column
     where: and(like(tasks.id, `${taskId}%`), eq(tasks.projectId, projectId)),
     
     // Line 310 - Same bug in dependency resolution
     where: and(like(tasks.id, `${dep}%`), eq(tasks.projectId, projectId)),
     ```

   - **`packages/core/src/agents/orchestrator-agent.ts`** (lines 65-79)
     - **Bug 1b root cause**: MAX_TOKENS is only 16384, too low for complex decompositions
     ```typescript
     const MODEL = process.env.ORCHESTRATOR_MODEL || 'claude-opus-4-6';
     const MAX_TOKENS = parseInt(process.env.CLAUDE_MAX_TOKENS || '16384', 10);
     const TEMPERATURE = 0;
     ```

   - **`packages/core/src/agents/utils/orchestrator-output-parser.ts`** (full file, 694 lines)
     - Contains `extractYamlContent()` function (lines 146-196) that extracts YAML from Claude's response
     - **Bug 1a root cause**: When response is truncated (hits max_tokens), the YAML code block closing ` ``` ` is missing, so regex `([\s\S]*?)\n\`\`\`` doesn't match
     - Fallback parser (lines 172-193) also fails on truncated YAML because it terminates when hitting non-YAML-looking lines
     - The `stripInformationalSections()` function (lines 512-540) strips `analysis`, `milestone_progress`, `summary` sections but doesn't help with truncation
     - Parser returns 0 tasks when YAML is incomplete, causing the "no actionable commands" escalation

4. Errors and fixes:
   - **Bug 1 (Truncated YAML → 0 tasks parsed)**: The orchestrator using Claude Opus produces 4000+ streaming chunks for complex project decompositions. With MAX_TOKENS=16384, the response gets truncated before YAML completes. The `extractYamlContent()` function can't find complete YAML code blocks (missing closing ```), and fallback parsers also fail on incomplete YAML. This caused 3 consecutive failures before the 4th attempt happened to complete within token limits. **Not yet fixed - in plan mode.**
   - **Bug 2 (UUID LIKE operator error)**: `operator does not exist: uuid ~~ unknown` occurs at line 59 and 310 of `orchestrator-command-executor.ts` when the orchestrator references tasks by short UUID prefixes (e.g., `"0bb68441"` instead of full UUID). ```markdown
# Fix: Orchestrator YAML Truncation + UUID LIKE Operator Bugs

## Bug 1: Truncated YAML → 0 Tasks Parsed

**Root cause:** The orchestrator (Claude Opus) produces 4000+ streaming chunks for complex project decompositions. With `MAX_TOKENS=16384`, the response gets truncated before the YAML block completes. The `extractYamlContent()` function in `orchestrator-output-parser.ts` can't find complete YAML (missing closing ` ``` `), and fallback parsers also fail on incomplete YAML. Result: 0 tasks parsed → "no actionable commands" → escalation after 3 failures.

**Fix (two parts):**

### 1a. Make parser handle truncated YAML gracefully
**File:** `packages/core/src/agents/utils/orchestrator-output-parser.ts`

- In `extractYamlContent()` (line ~146): Add a fallback that, when no closing ` ``` ` is found, takes content from the opening ` ```yaml ` to the end of the string
- Attempt to parse this potentially-incomplete YAML — if the last entry is malformed, drop it and keep the rest
- This way even a truncated response yields partial tasks instead of zero

### 1b. Increase MAX_TOKENS for orchestrator
**File:** `packages/core/src/agents/orchestrator-agent.ts`

- Add `ORCHESTRATOR_MAX_TOKENS` env var override (default 32768) separate from the general `CLAUDE_MAX_TOKENS`
- The orchestrator is the only agent that produces massive structured YAML output; other agents don't need this

---

## Bug 2: UUID LIKE Operator Error

**Root cause:** `resolveTaskIdWithLookup()` in `orchestrator-command-executor.ts` uses `like(tasks.id, ...)` at lines 59 and 310. PostgreSQL UUID type doesn't support the `~~` (LIKE) operator. This crashes when the orchestrator references tasks by short UUID prefixes.

**Fix:**
**File:** `packages/core/src/agents/utils/orchestrator-command-executor.ts`

- Replace `like(tasks.id, ...)` with `sql` template that casts UUID to text first:
  ```typescript
  sql`${tasks.id}::text LIKE ${taskId + '%'}`
  ```
- Apply at both line 59 and line 310

---

## Files Changed
1. `packages/core/src/agents/utils/orchestrator-output-parser.ts` — truncated YAML handling
2. `packages/core/src/agents/orchestrator-agent.ts` — orchestrator-specific MAX_TOKENS
3. `packages/core/src/agents/utils/orchestrator-command-executor.ts` — UUID cast to text for LIKE

## Testing
- Update existing parser tests to cover truncated YAML input
- Update command executor tests to verify UUID prefix resolution works with cast
- Run `pnpm test:all` to verify no regressions

```

### Assistant
Here's exactly what's happening and why the orchestrator runs 6 times:

## The Question-Answer Loop

The orchestrator runs in a **question → re-queue → re-run** cycle. Here's the flow:

1. **Run 1:** Orchestrator calls Claude API, streams ~4000 chunks, produces a response. If the response contains questions (parsed from YAML), it creates question records in the DB and sets the task to `waiting_human` status (line 384).

2. **Human answers a question** via the PATCH `/api/questions/[id]` endpoint. This endpoint:
   - Saves the answer to the question record
   - Appends the Q&A pair to `task.context.answeredQuestions` (line 97-100)
   - **Re-queues the entire task** back to the orchestrator BullMQ queue (line 119)

3. **Run 2:** The orchestrator picks up the re-queued job. It rebuilds the **full prompt** from scratch — system prompt (skills), orchestrator context (project state), and user prompt (including all answered questions). Then it calls Claude API again, streams another ~4000 chunks. If Claude asks more questions, the cycle repeats.

4. **Runs 3-6:** Same cycle. Each time a question is answered, the entire orchestrator pipeline runs from scratch — skill loading, context building, full Claude API call with streaming.

## Why the chunks repeat every time

The orchestrator has **no conversation memory**. Look at lines 266-277: it sends a single `messages` array with one `user` message. There's no multi-turn conversation. Every run is a **brand new API call** with the full prompt reconstructed from scratch.

The answered questions are injected into the prompt via `buildPrompt()` (lines 165-175) as a "Previously Asked Questions" section, but Claude still has to re-process the entire system prompt, skills, context, and produce a full new response. That's why you see 4000+ chunks streaming each time.

## The cost implications

Each run costs:
- Full input token charge (system prompt + skills + context + prompt + all previous Q&As)
- Full output token charge (~4000 chunks worth)
- The input grows each iteration because `answeredQuestions` accumulates

With 6 runs, you're paying for 6 full Opus API calls. And the input cost compounds because each iteration includes all previous Q&A pairs.

## Is this expected behavior?

It's **by design** but arguably wasteful. The architecture intentionally makes each orchestrator run stateless — it rebuilds everything from scratch. The upside is simplicity and fault tolerance (no conversation state to corrupt). The downside is massive token waste when the orchestrator asks multiple rounds of questions.

The 5-question circuit breaker (lines 230-253) exists precisely because this pattern is expensive — it caps the loop at 5 rounds before escalating.

## The Honest Cost Breakdown First

Before designing solutions, let's look at where money actually goes on 6 orchestrator runs (Opus pricing):

| Component | Tokens/run | 6 runs total | $/M tokens | Cost |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Input: Skills (cached after run 1)** | ~4,000 | ~4,000 + 5×400 | $15 / $1.50 | ~$0.06 |
| **Input: Context + prompt** | ~2,000 | ~12,000 | $15 | ~$0.18 |
| **Output (the real killer)** | ~6,000-8,000 | ~36,000-48,000 | **$75** | **~$2.70-3.60** |

**Output tokens are 85-90% of your bill.** The input caching Anthropic already provides (which you've implemented) handles the skill files. The orchestrator context is only 600-800 tokens — peanuts.

The user's instinct to "avoid repeating data collection" is right directionally, but the real savings come from **not making Claude produce a full YAML decomposition from scratch 6 times**. The input is cheap. The output is what bleeds you dry.

---

## Why 6 Runs Happened (Be Honest About Root Causes)

Looking at your logs, the 6 runs weren't all legitimate question rounds:

1. **Runs 1-3**: YAML truncated at max_tokens=16384 → parser returns 0 tasks → "no actionable commands" → escalated to human → human answers → re-queued → **full re-run**
2. **Run 4**: Finally produced parseable output (questions)
3. **Run 5-6**: Question-answer loop + UUID LIKE crash

**At least 3 of those 6 runs were caused by bugs, not by the question loop.** Fixing the YAML truncation bug alone would cut your runs in half. That's $1.50+ saved per project with zero architectural change.

---

## Multi-Turn Conversation: The Real Analysis

Now to your actual question. Yes, you can make the orchestrator stateful. Here are the three approaches, ranked by pragmatism:

### Approach A: Multi-Turn Messages (Store conversation in Redis/DB)

Store the Claude API messages array between runs. Instead of a single `user` message each time, you'd have:

```
Run 1: [user: "decompose this project"] → [assistant: "here are questions..."]
Run 2: [user: "decompose..."][assistant: "questions..."][user: "here are answers..."] → [assistant: "tasks..."]
```

**Pros:**
- Claude sees its own previous reasoning — won't re-derive the same analysis
- Output drops dramatically on run 2+ (incremental response vs. full re-decomposition)
- Estimated output savings: **60-80% on subsequent runs**

**Cons:**
- Context window grows every turn. After 6 turns with Opus responses, you could hit 50K+ tokens of conversation history
- Anthropic prompt caching breaks. Cache prefix matching requires identical message prefixes. Any change to early messages (like updated project state) invalidates the cache on ALL subsequent messages
- **Stale state risk**: Between run 2 and run 3, other tasks may have completed, locks changed, etc. The orchestrator's cached conversation reflects OLD project state
- Error propagation: If run 2's reasoning was flawed, run 3 builds on that flawed reasoning

**Verdict: Dangerous.** The stale state problem is a deal-breaker for a task orchestrator. It MUST see current project state to make correct assignment decisions. A multi-turn conversation that shows stale task statuses will produce wrong commands.

### Approach B: Session Summary + Delta (Recommended)

After each orchestrator run, extract a **compact session summary** that captures decisions made, not the full conversation. Next run gets:

1. Skills (cached, same as today)
2. **Session summary** (~200-400 tokens): "Previously decided: 15 tasks planned for phases 1-3. Asked about tech stack, user chose React+Next.js. Asked about hosting, user chose Vercel."
3. Current project state (fresh, same as today)
4. Only NEW information since last run

**Implementation:**
- After each run, use a cheap model (Haiku) to extract a structured session summary from the orchestrator's response
- Store summary in task `context.sessionSummary` (already have context field in DB)
- On re-run, inject summary into prompt as "Session State" section
- The orchestrator sees fresh project state BUT also knows what it previously decided

**Pros:**
- Output drops on subsequent runs (Claude knows what it decided, doesn't re-derive)
- No stale state — current project state is always fresh
- Prompt caching still works (skills are still the stable prefix)
- Cheap to implement — no new infrastructure, just a prompt section and a Haiku call
- Summary is ~200-400 tokens vs. 6,000+ tokens of raw conversation history

**Cons:**
- Haiku summary extraction adds ~$0.001 per run (negligible)
- Summary might lose subtle context (mitigated by keeping it structured)
- Adds ~50ms latency for the Haiku call

**Estimated savings on a 6-run scenario:**

| | Current | With Session Summary |
|---|---|---|
| Runs needed (bug fixes applied) | 3 | 3 |
| Output tokens per run | ~7,000 | Run 1: 7,000, Run 2-3: ~2,000 |
| Total output tokens | ~21,000 | ~11,000 |
| Output cost | ~$1.58 | **~$0.83** |
| **Savings** | | **~47%** |

### Approach C: Orchestrator Skill Prompt Optimization (The Free Win)

Before adding infrastructure, ask: **why is the orchestrator asking 6 rounds of questions?**

Looking at your skill files, the orchestrator is instructed to ask clarifying questions when uncertain. For a "Premium Car Brand Marketing Website," there's arguably enough in the scope document to decompose without asking anything. The scoper agent already gathered requirements.

**Fix the skill prompt** to:
- Default to making reasonable assumptions instead of asking
- Only ask questions that are truly blocking (can't proceed without answer)
- When scope data exists, treat it as authoritative — don't re-validate

This could reduce legitimate question rounds from 3-4 to 0-1. That's a **67-100% reduction in re-runs** with zero code changes.

---

## My Recommendation: Layered Approach

In order of priority and ROI:

### Phase 1: Fix the bugs (immediate, free)
- YAML truncation handling → eliminates 2-3 wasted runs per complex project
- UUID LIKE cast → eliminates the crash
- **ROI: Highest. Eliminates ~50% of unnecessary runs.**

### Phase 2: Skill prompt tightening (immediate, free)
- Make the orchestrator less question-happy when scope data exists
- **ROI: High. Eliminates 1-2 question rounds per project.**

### Phase 3: Session Summary (moderate effort, high value)
- Implement the summary extraction + injection pattern
- Store in existing `task.context` field — no schema change needed
- Use Haiku for extraction (~$0.001 per call)
- **ROI: Good. Cuts output tokens ~47% on remaining multi-run scenarios.**

### Phase 4: MAX_TOKENS increase (trivial, safety net)
- Bump orchestrator to 32768 tokens
- Prevents truncation on complex decompositions
- **ROI: Safety net, not a cost reduction.**

### What I Would NOT Do

- **Multi-turn conversation storage**: Stale state risk too high for an orchestrator that needs to make real-time decisions about task assignment
- **Redis session caching of full context**: Over-engineering for the token counts involved. The orchestrator context is 600-800 tokens — caching it saves $0.01 per run
- **Custom embedding/RAG for session history**: Way too heavy for this use case. You're not searching through millions of documents, you're resuming a 3-turn conversation

```
function extractYamlContent(responseText: string): string | null {
  // Try to find YAML in markdown code block first (complete block)
  const yamlBlockRegex = /```(?:yaml|yml)\s*\n([\s\S]*?)\n```/i;
  const match = responseText.match(yamlBlockRegex);
  if (match && match[1]) {
    return match[1].trim();
  }

  // Truncation-aware fallback: opening ```yaml exists but no closing ```
  // This happens when Claude's response hits max_tokens and gets cut off mid-YAML.
  const truncatedYamlRegex = /```(?:yaml|yml)\s*\n([\s\S]+)$/i;
  const truncatedMatch = responseText.match(truncatedYamlRegex);
  if (truncatedMatch && truncatedMatch[1]) {
    const content = truncatedMatch[1].trim();
    // Validate it actually looks like YAML (not just random text after ```)
    if (content.includes(':') && !content.startsWith('{')) {
      console.warn('[OrchestratorParser] YAML block appears truncated (no closing ```), attempting partial parse');
      return repairTruncatedYaml(content);
    }
  }

  // Try generic code block
  const genericBlockRegex = /```\s*\n([\s\S]*?)\n```/;
  const genericMatch = responseText.match(genericBlockRegex);
  if (genericMatch && genericMatch[1]) {
    // Check if it looks like YAML (has key: value patterns)
    const content = genericMatch[1].trim();
    if (content.includes(':') && !content.startsWith('{')) {
      return content;
    }
  }
```

**New:**

// ============================================================================
// YAML Extraction
// ============================================================================

/**
 * Repair truncated YAML by dropping the last incomplete entry.
 * When Claude's response is cut off mid-YAML, the last task/question/entry
 * is likely incomplete. We find the last complete top-level list item and
 * drop everything after it.
 */
function repairTruncatedYaml(yaml: string): string {
  const lines = yaml.split('\n');

  // Walk backwards to find the last complete top-level list item boundary.
  // Top-level list items start with "  - " (2-space indent + dash) under
  // a section key like "tasks:", "questions:", etc.
  // We look for the last "  - " that is followed by at least one complete
  // key-value pair (i.e., next "  - " or section key exists after it).
  let lastCompleteItemEnd = -1;

  for (let i = lines.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
    const line = lines[i];
    // Found a top-level list item start (e.g., "  - name: ...")
    if (/^  - \w+:/.test(line)) {
      // This item is the last one and likely truncated — drop it.
      // The item BEFORE this one (if any) was complete.
      lastCompleteItemEnd = i;
      break;
    }
  }

  if (lastCompleteItemEnd > 0) {
    const repaired = lines.slice(0, lastCompleteItemEnd).join('\n').trim();
    console.warn(`[OrchestratorParser] Dropped truncated last entry, keeping ${lastCompleteItemEnd} lines of ${lines.length}`);
    return repaired;
  }

  // Can't find a safe cut point — return as-is and let YAML parser try
  return yaml;
}

/**
 * Extract YAML content from response text.
 * Handles markdown code blocks (```yaml ... ```) and raw YAML.
```

**Content:**
```typescript
/**
 * Session Summary Extractor
 *
 * After each orchestrator run, extracts a compact summary of decisions made,
 * tasks planned, and questions asked. This summary is stored in task context
 * and injected on re-runs so Claude can continue from where it left off
 * without re-deriving everything from scratch.
 *
 * Uses Haiku for extraction (~$0.001 per call) to minimize overhead.
 */

import Anthropic from '@anthropic-ai/sdk';

const SUMMARY_MODEL = 'claude-haiku-4-5-20251001';
const SUMMARY_MAX_TOKENS = 1024;

let anthropicClient: Anthropic | null = null;

function getClient(): Anthropic {
  if (!anthropicClient) {
    const apiKey = process.env.ANTHROPIC_API_KEY;
    if (!apiKey) throw new Error('ANTHROPIC_API_KEY required for session summary');
    anthropicClient = new Anthropic({ apiKey });
  }
  return anthropicClient;
}

export interface SessionSummary {
  /** Compact summary of decisions, plans, and open items */
  summary: string;
  /** Number of tasks the orchestrator planned/created */
  tasksPlanned: number;
  /** Number of questions asked to the human */
  questionsAsked: number;
  /** Timestamp of this summary */
  timestamp: string;
  /** Token cost of the summary extraction */
  extractionTokens: { input: number; output: number };
}

/**
 * Extract a compact session summary from an orchestrator's response.
 * Returns null if extraction fails (non-blocking — orchestrator continues without summary).
 */
export async function extractSessionSummary(
  orchestratorResponse: string,
  taskName: string,
  previousSummary?: string,
): Promise<SessionSummary | null> {
  try {
    const client = getClient();

    const prompt = buildExtractionPrompt(orchestratorResponse, taskName, previousSummary);

    const response = await client.messages.create({
      model: SUMMARY_MODEL,
      max_tokens: SUMMARY_MAX_TOKENS,
      temperature: 0,
      messages: [{ role: 'user', content: prompt }],
    });

    const text = response.content.find(b => b.type === 'text');
    if (!text || text.type !== 'text') return null;

    const summary = text.text.trim();

    // Count tasks and questions from the summary text (rough heuristic)
    const tasksPlanned = (summary.match(/task/gi) || []).length;
    const questionsAsked = (summary.match(/question|asked|clarif/gi) || []).length;

    return {
      summary,
      tasksPlanned,
      questionsAsked,
      timestamp: new Date().toISOString(),
      extractionTokens: {
        input: response.usage?.input_tokens ?? 0,
        output: response.usage?.output_tokens ?? 0,
      },
    };
  } catch (err) {
    console.error('[SessionSummary] Extraction failed (non-blocking):', err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err));
    return null;
  }
}

function buildExtractionPrompt(
  orchestratorResponse: string,
  taskName: string,
  previousSummary?: string,
): string {
  const parts: string[] = [];

  parts.push('Extract a compact session summary from this orchestrator response. The summary will be used in a future prompt so the orchestrator can continue from where it left off.');
  parts.push('');

  if (previousSummary) {
    parts.push('## Previous Session Summary');
    parts.push(previousSummary);
    parts.push('');
    parts.push('Build on the previous summary — merge, don\'t duplicate.');
    parts.push('');
  }

  parts.push(`## Task: ${taskName}`);
  parts.push('');
  parts.push('## Orchestrator Response');
  // Truncate to keep Haiku call cheap — first 4000 chars is enough for summary
  parts.push(orchestratorResponse.slice(0, 4000));
  parts.push('');
  parts.push('## Instructions');
  parts.push('Write a concise summary (max 300 words) covering:');
  parts.push('1. **Decisions made**: Architecture choices, tech stack, task breakdown strategy');
  parts.push('2. **Tasks created**: List task names and their assigned agent types');
  parts.push('3. **Questions asked**: What was asked of the human and why');
  parts.push('4. **Open items**: What still needs to be decided or done next');
  parts.push('');
  parts.push('Write in plain text, no markdown headers. Be factual and specific — names, counts, agent types. Skip analysis/reasoning, just state what happened.');

  return parts.join('\n');
}

/**
 * Build the session context block for injection into the orchestrator prompt.
 * Returns empty string if no session summary exists.
 */
export function buildSessionContextBlock(sessionSummary: string | undefined): string {
  if (!sessionSummary) return '';

  return [
    '\n**Session State (your previous decisions — continue from here, do NOT re-derive):**',
    sessionSummary,
    '',
    '**IMPORTANT:** The above is what you previously decided. Build on these decisions. Do NOT start over or re-analyze from scratch. Focus only on what\'s new or changed since your last response.',
    '',
  ].join('\n');
}

```

// ============================================================================
// Truncated YAML handling (Bug #1: max_tokens truncation)
// ============================================================================

describe('Truncated YAML handling', () => {
  it('parses truncated YAML with no closing ``` — keeps complete tasks, drops incomplete last one', () => {
    // Simulates Claude hitting max_tokens mid-YAML: opening ```yaml but no closing ```
    const yaml = [
      'Here is my analysis of the project.',
      '',
      '```yaml',
      'action: decompose_and_assign',
      'tasks:',
      '  - name: "Setup project structure"',
      '    description: "Initialize Next.js project"',
      '    agent: backend',
      '    priority: high',
      '    dependencies: []',
      '  - name: "Create database schema"',
      '    description: "Design and implement the database"',
      '    agent: backend',
      '    priority: high',
      '    dependencies: []',
      '  - name: "Build API endpoints"',
      '    description: "Create REST API for the',  // Truncated mid-value
    ].join('\n');

    const result = parseOrchestratorOutput(yaml);

    expect(result.success).toBe(true);
    expect(result.action).toBe('decompose_and_assign');
    // Should have the 2 complete tasks, not the truncated 3rd
    expect(result.tasks.length).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(2);
    expect(result.tasks[0].name).toBe('Setup project structure');
    expect(result.tasks[1].name).toBe('Create database schema');
  });

  it('parses truncated YAML with complete tasks but no closing ```', () => {
    const yaml = [
      '```yaml',
      'action: request_clarification',
      'questions:',
      '  - question: "What tech stack should we use?"',
      '    category: technical',
      '    priority: critical',
      '  - question: "Should we support mobile?"',
      '    category: scope',
      '    priority: high',
      // No closing ``` — truncated but all entries are complete
    ].join('\n');

    const result = parseOrchestratorOutput(yaml);

    expect(result.success).toBe(true);
    expect(result.action).toBe('request_clarification');
    expect(result.questions.length).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(1);
    expect(result.questions[0].question).toBe('What tech stack should we use?');
  });

  it('returns failure when truncation leaves no parseable content', () => {
    const yaml = [
      '```yaml',
      'action: decompose_and_assign',
      'tasks:',
      '  - na',  // Truncated immediately
    ].join('\n');

    const result = parseOrchestratorOutput(yaml);

    // Even if partial parse fails, it shouldn't crash
    // May succeed with 0 tasks or fail gracefully
    expect(result).toBeDefined();
  });

  it('handles complete YAML normally (no regression)', () => {
    const yaml = [
      '```yaml',
      'action: decompose_and_assign',
      'tasks:',
      '  - name: "Build feature"',
      '    description: "Implement the feature"',
      '    agent: backend',
      '    priority: medium',
      '    dependencies: []',
      '```',
    ].join('\n');

    const result = parseOrchestratorOutput(yaml);

    expect(result.success).toBe(true);
    expect(result.tasks).toHaveLength(1);
    expect(result.tasks[0].name).toBe('Build feature');
  });
});
